# Second opinion (Phase 4.6), M6 T31: EmulationStation's other pages, in pixelelated's EmulationStation fork

You are the one independent reviewer of a read-only renovation audit, read under a **new-functionality and brittleness lens**. Its subject is the 76 files under `es-app/src/guis/` other than GuiMenu, the cloud pages, the RetroAchievements pages, the offline scan and the Wi-Fi page. They are read at the EmulationStation commit `16dbac5211`, which the distribution pins.
- **Twelve files are the fork's.** Three it added (`GuiScraperRun.cpp`/`.h`, the SCRAPING GAMES page; `WifiScanProcess.h`, an argv executor). Nine it modified: the save state manager, SCRAPER, BIOS CHECK, the `GuiSettings` base, the image viewer and the game options menu.
- **Sixty-four are byte-identical to ROCKNIX's master.** Upstream pages (Moonlight, netplay, Bluetooth, the metadata editor, the key mapping editor, the stores...) are read for brittleness. A defect in one is still closed when the fix is contained.

**What ships.** An immutable handheld OS that a person updates in place, keeping `/storage`.
- EmulationStation is its interface, at 640x480 on the smallest panel, run as root.
- The two stores are `system.cfg` (`SystemConf`, the launch and boot scripts' store) and `es_settings.cfg` (`Settings`, EmulationStation's own).
- A settings page is a `GuiSettings`: its rows apply on close through save functions.
- The rules the code answers to are named in `03` § 3.2.

**What you get.**
- The first reader is another lab's model. You get its `02-forward-audit.md`, `03-retrospective.md` and `04-analysis.md` whole. `02`'s tables are generated by tools whose methods are stated in `02` beside them.
- Then context: the brief's questions and the register rows the code cites.
- Then the guest window's records and the host harnesses' output (`stage-c/runs.txt`).
- Then **the twelve fork-touched files whole, line-numbered**.
- Then **line-numbered excerpts of the upstream pages**, each cited range with eight lines of context. The rest of the 64 is trimmed (D-WORKFLOW-195), and the packet's manifest names every range sent.
- Then excerpts of the neighbours the leads turn on: `GuiMsgBox.cpp`, `OptionListComponent.h`, `Settings.cpp`, `SystemConf.cpp`, `AtomicFileUtil.cpp`, `ApiSystem.cpp`, `SaveState.cpp`, `SaveStateRepository.cpp`, `DisplayAspect.cpp`, `SystemData.cpp`, `FileSystemUtil.cpp`.
- **When a claim needs lines outside the packet, say `outside the packet` and do not guess.**

**Masking.** Every section passed through a mask that replaces a value after `key=`, `key:`, `token=`, `user=`, `pass...=` or `psk=` with `***`. A `***` is the mask, not the code. The packet was scanned for credential shapes before it left; no hit was a value.

**Your job is to refute, narrow or confirm, against the code: not to agree.** Cite `file:line` for every claim, and say plainly where the first reader is wrong. Its line numbers are the pin's.

**What was run.** Nothing can be run for you.
- Five host harnesses compiled the pin's own functions over doubles. Each failed at the pin and passed a constructed control (`stage-c/runs.txt`).
- Six generators produced `02`'s tables. Each asserts its counts and was seen to fail on a constructed drift.
- A guest window ran an image whose EmulationStation is a later composition over the pin (it carries the pin's literals and later ones). A guest proof is the image's, and `02` § The guest cycle says which.

Answer these first, in this order:

1. **The base (Q2; L-01, L-02, L-03, L-16).**
   - Is L-01 right: START (CLOSE) deletes the pages without `close()`, so no save function or `onFinalize` runs? Is anything relying on that, so that closing each page through `close()` would break it (an `onFinalize` that pushes a page, a page that is not a `GuiSettings`)?
   - Is L-02 right: the LED page's colour never reaches `system.cfg`?
   - Is L-03 right, and does anything else on that page apply on close?
   - Is L-16's reading of `Settings` (a default counted as a change) right?
2. **The fork's pages (Q3, Q4; L-08 to L-15, L-26, L-27).**
   - Is any claim about the save state manager, the scrape page or BIOS CHECK wrong?
   - Is COPY TO FREE SLOT's silence (L-13) right on both paths?
   - Is anything on the scrape page's thread or input wrong beyond L-10?
   - Is L-26 right that BIOS CHECK draws no help bar under full-screen menus?
3. **The commands (Q6; L-05, L-06).**
   - Is any other command in the slice built from a string a player, a file or the network supplies, unquoted?
   - Is `WifiScanProcess.h` sound as an executor?
4. **The dialogs (Q1; L-19 to L-21).** Is the landing rule read right (`GuiMsgBox.cpp:116-134`)? Is any landing on a destructive or acting choice missed?
5. **The threads (Q3; L-17, L-18, L-22).**
   - Is L-22's race real as the code stands?
   - Is any `GuiLoading` completion or detached thread unsound beyond #1212's shape?
6. **The words and the map (Q5, Q7; L-07, L-11, L-23, L-24).** Is anything recorded here wrong?

Then list, as **new items**, any defect the first reader missed. Give each its `file:line`, its family, and what would prove it. The families are:
- a state write that can lose the record;
- a check whose failure reads as success;
- a command built from a player's string;
- a credential's sink;
- a dialog whose back lands on the acting choice;
- work on the interface thread;
- a page with no help bar;
- a player sentence outside the vocabulary.

**The shape of the answer.** One item per lead or finding, numbered `EP-1`, `EP-2`, ... Each item:
- names the lead (or `new`);
- gives a verdict: `confirmed`, `narrowed`, `refuted` or `new`;
- gives the evidence at `file:line`;
- says what the first reader should change, if anything.

Close with the items you would rank highest, and why.

What each page runs, and on which thread, beyond the census:
- The process starts are all on the interface thread with no bound: `GuiMoonlight`'s 5 `executeScriptLegacy` and 3 `runSystemCommand`, the LED page's one per slider step (L-02), and `GuiControllersSettings`' 2. Moonlight's `popen ("moonlight -v")` at `:42` is the version probe of its client.
- The 18 `GuiLoading<` run their work on a worker and their completion on the interface thread, holding the page's raw `this` (#1212's shape, T21's grading), over L-22's race.
- The 18 `std::thread` mentions are `GuiLoading.h`'s 2, `GuiUpdate`'s 6 (the OS update, D-UI-079's named card), `GuiBluetoothPair`'s 2, `GuiNetPlay`'s 2, `GuiBackup`'s, `GuiInstall`'s and `GuiAutoScrape`'s (not built).

- Every YES / NO box lands on NO: 17 sites. The fork's two (`GuiSaveState.cpp:449`, `GuiScraperRun.cpp:112`) and upstream's fifteen, `GuiScraperStart.cpp:449` and `:467` among them. The two THIS WILL DELETE boxes (`GuiGameOptions.cpp:253`, `GuiMetaDataEd.cpp:365`) land on NO; on the guest, B on DELETE GAME and on the manager's DELETE left the files (G07). proved: guest.
- WRITE / CANCEL (`GuiGameOptions.cpp:274`) lands on CANCEL. CLOSE (`:289`) is a single button.
- **Three landings press the choice that acts** (proved: tree):
  - `GuiControllersSettings.cpp:76` is OK / CANCEL: back presses **OK** and starts the controller mapping (L-19);
  - `GuiMetaDataEd.cpp:524` is YES / NO / CANCEL: back presses **NO**, which saves the edits without the copies (L-20);
  - the two SAVE CHANGES? boxes (`GuiMetaDataEd.cpp:629`, `GuiKeyMappingEditor.cpp:497`) land on **NO**, which discards the edits (L-21).
- **The words:**
  - FAILED (`GuiNetPlay.cpp:336`, with HttpReq's error text after it);
  - four sentence-case English boxes in `GuiMoonlight.cpp`, `Succesfully` misspelt;
  - the manager's `<busy>` and `<busyNow>` are its composed refusals;
  - one message per family across pages, identical at every site: ARE YOU SURE YOU WANT TO DELETE THIS ITEM? (4), THIS WILL DELETE THE ACTUAL GAME FILE(S)! (2), YOU ARE NOT CONNECTED TO A NETWORK (3), FINISHED (3; `GuiAutoScrape`'s is not built).

- **Text rows:**
  - The scraper's six account rows go to `Settings`, the three secrets with `password = true`.
  - The netplay and Moonlight rows go to SystemConf. **PLAYER PASSWORD (`GuiGameOptions.cpp:204`) is `password = false`** (L-04).
  - `updateVal` (`GuiSettings.cpp:218-238`) writes to the store when the editor's OK is pressed, empty or not (#1185, cited). No row here guards an untouched editor; the account rows' mode is #1193's (cited).
- **Two-line rows:** the 8 `addWithDescription` calls. The `grep`'s 10 lines include two commented out (`GuiFavoriteMusicSelector.cpp:45-46`).
- **Reboot flags:** none set or named in the slice.
- **The metadata editor's popups** (`GuiMetaDataEd.cpp:333-341`) are upstream's own rows and bind no store; it saves through its own `save()`.

## The merge debt

- **The bases:** `cada856d86` is the merge base with `rocknix/master` (`f6c542872e`). `bccd715707` is its parent and the base the earlier briefs used (`rev-list --count` 1). `upstream/master` in the ES repository is a stale copy and was not read.
- **Upstream's side:** 14 commits past the base, 8 of them touching `guis/`, **0 touching a slice file** (GuiMenu's 152/2 and 1/0 are T26's). The 64 upstream files are at `rocknix/master`'s bytes today.

## Positives

- `WifiScanProcess.h`, read whole, is the argv model #1192 names:
  - `posix_spawnp` in its own process group, with a relocated low pipe end;
  - a 256 KiB cap and a deadline on the read, and a 2 s reap;
  - `-1` for every failure, and lines only on exit 0.
- Every YES / NO box lands on NO (17 of 17), and the fork's confirmations are YES first (proved: guest, G07, for DELETE GAME and the manager).
- The three `clearButtons(` sites rebuild from page methods, none from a button's own lambda.
- `GuiScraperRun`'s `delete this` at `:101` is the page deleting itself from its own input. Its help bar is drawn under full-screen menus (`:173-174`), and the spinner is never a bar.
- The manager's placeholders are never null (`SaveStateRepository.cpp:280-294`). Its help bar is drawn at 640x480 and follows the cursor (G06: BACK / LAUNCH on START NEW GAME; BACK / DELETE / COPY TO FREE SLOT / LAUNCH on a slot).
- `GuiAnalogSticksLedControls`' parse is guarded by the token count.
- No `system(`; no reboot flag; 0 non-ASCII comments.
- The French: 706 of 706 against `bccd715707`, 703 of 703 against `cada856d86`.

## Corrections to the brief

1. `:830`'s non-ASCII adjacency grep is a basic-regex error (accepted by the coordinator, 12:09 UTC).
2. `popen(` 0 → **1** (`GuiMoonlight.cpp:42`, `popen (` with a space).
3. `GuiKeyboardLayout.cpp:164` writes `/tmp/kblayout-N.svg`, a scratch render on tmpfs, not a file on the card.
4. The LED page's `tokens[6]` lead is guarded (`tokens.size() == 7`).
5. "`save()` rewrites `es_settings.cfg` twice on every visit" holds on a first visit, but not for the reason given: `Settings` counts a default as a change. Stage B's own first correction ("only on a change") was wrong in effect, and the guest corrected it (L-16).
6. `GuiAutoScrape` is not built (L-23).
7. The dialogs counted by landing replace the three-line heuristic: OK 30, NO 18, CANCEL 1, CLOSE 1.

## What T32 to T37 inherit

- **The provenance table** (§ Coverage): 3 / 9 / 64 against `cada856d86`, the 64 at `rocknix/master`'s bytes.
- **The dialog method:** the first button is read whole, never by a three-line window. The landing comes from `GuiMsgBox.cpp:116-134`'s rule, which `t31-dialogs.py` asserts from the source.
- **The title-shape table** (§ The titles). The checker's three regexes see 12 of 37 pages; a raw literal (MOONLIGHT) and a computed title (`GuiBios`' DETAILS, `GuiSettings(window, displayName)`) are invisible to it.
- **The cached-read table** (§ The reads): the scraper, RetroArch and the player are the writers of what these pages ask `exists` about.
- **The four tab-strip pages**, with their lines: `GuiThemeInstaller`, `GuiBatoceraStore`, `GuiKeyMappingEditor` (`:150-156`), `GuiKeyboardtopads` (`:78`).
- **The base's two traps:**
  - START drops what a page did not save (L-01);
  - a page with no save function never writes `system.cfg`, whatever it `set` (L-02).



## The first reader's 03-retrospective.md (whole)

# Retrospective audit — M6.P2 T31, EmulationStation's other pages (#678)

This was written by the first reader (Opus 5.5) after stages A and B and the guest window, before any seat saw the packet. Line numbers are the pin's (`16dbac5211`). The leads are `02`'s (L-01 to L-27).

## 3.1 Architectural coherence

- **One page, one file.** All 37 pages are each their own class in their own file. The fork's three new pages and nine changes sit where the pages already lived. Nothing here is a second copy of a GuiMenu page.
- **Two page shapes, and the help bar follows the shape.**
  - The `GuiSettings` pages get their bar from `MenuComponent`.
  - The full-screen `GuiComponent` pages must draw their own bar under full-screen menus. The fork's save state manager and scrape page do (`GuiSaveState.cpp:397-398`, `GuiScraperRun.cpp:173-174`); the fork-rebuilt BIOS CHECK does not (L-26, guest).
- **`GuiSettings` is the base every settings page stands on, and it has two exits.**
  - BACK goes through `close()`: the save functions, `saveFile`, `saveSystemConf`, then `onFinalize`.
  - START, CLOSE on the bar, deletes the pages without any of it (L-01).

  A page whose writes all happen in save functions loses them on START. A page that writes at once (`updateVal`, the LED page) keeps them in memory but writes the files only if some later `save()` runs (L-02). The base's contract is "apply on close", and one of its two closes does not apply.
- **The long jobs follow D-UI-078 where the fork works.** SCRAPING GAMES is a fourth-tier page with CANCEL; the hasher and the OS update keep upstream's cards (D-UI-079). The manager's DELETE runs on a worker (D-UI-073), and its COPY still runs on the interface thread (L-13).
- **`GuiLoading` is the async primitive of 18 call sites here and 41 lines in the tree.** It runs the work on a thread and the completion on the interface thread, and its done flag is a plain `bool` (L-22, ThreadSanitizer).

## 3.2 Project conformance

- **`least-surprise.md`:**
  - A COPY that worked and one that failed leave the same frame (L-13, guest).
  - START drops choices that B keeps (L-01, guest).
  - UNPAIR says it cleared what it did not (L-06, guest).
  - A relay change silently undoes ENABLE NETPLAY (L-03, guest).
- **`es-player-text.md` § Outcome vocabulary:**
  - SKIPPED is reserved for 69, 75 and the launch cancel, and the scrape page adds a fourth reason (L-08, for the pause).
  - An all-errored run says COULDN'T FINISH with no why and no recovery (L-09).
  - FAILED and HttpReq's own text reach the player on the netplay page (upstream's, recorded).
- **`es-player-text.md` § Every fork string ships in English and French:** the fork's strings all have French (706/706). The BIOS summary composes them in English order (L-11, guest). Moonlight's title is outside `_()` altogether (L-07, guest).
- **`es-ui-style-guide.md` § Confirmations:** YES / NO always lands on NO. OK / CANCEL lands on OK, the acting choice (L-19). Two SAVE CHANGES? boxes land on the discarding NO (L-21). One three-button box lands on a saving NO (L-20). All three are upstream's.
- **`es-native-ui.md` § Core patterns ("a file another process writes is read uncached"):** the twelve `exists` sites are all cached. Their writers are the scraper, RetroArch and the player (§ The reads), so a remembered miss can hide what a scrape just fetched. The guest positive was not reached within the bound (G11).
- **`es-code-traps.md` (the window paragraph):** BIOS CHECK is a full-screen page with no `renderHelpPromptsEarly` (L-26).
- **D-UI-039:** BIOS CHECK and SCRAPING GAMES are the fork's pages, and neither is in the map. Moonlight's page is in neither place either, and the checker cannot see it (L-24, L-07).
- **`engineering-practices.md` § Guards must fail closed:** `isFlockHeld` fails closed, with words that blame a sync (L-14).

## 3.3 Spec fidelity

The brief's figures held at their hashes (`01` § Scope, `02` § The runs), with seven corrections (`02` § Corrections to the brief), one of them the coordinator's own erratum (`:830`). Of the brief's leads:
- **Confirmed at their lines:** L-08 to L-15, L-17 and L-18.
- **Corrected:**
  - the LED parse is guarded;
  - `GuiKeyboardLayout.cpp:164` writes to `/tmp`;
  - the tab-switch writes happen for a reason other than the one the brief gave (L-16).
- **New:** L-01 to L-07, L-19 to L-23, L-26 and L-27.

## 3.4 Platform conformance

- **The image is M7's composition over the pin** (G01: both literals). Every guest verdict is the image's. The pages read here carry no M7 change that the frames contradict.
- **640x480 menus are full-screen:**
  - the manager draws its bar and shows its last tile (G06);
  - the scrape page's frames were not reached offline (G04);
  - BIOS CHECK draws no bar (G05).
- **ES runs as root** (`id -u` 0), so L-14's EACCES path is hard to reach on the image.
- **NETPLAY SETTINGS appears in GAME SETTINGS once a loaded system supports netplay** (`SystemData::isNetplayActivated`, `SystemData.cpp:2069-2072`). On the guest that was the Game Boy system G06 seeded: the row was absent at G05 and present at G12. That is the gate, not a defect.

## 3.5 Cross-system interaction audit

### Interaction: START x `GuiSettings::close` x every page's save functions

START's handler bypasses `close()`, and the pages built on `GuiSettings` depend on `close()` for their writes. That covers the fork's cloud pages (T26's) as much as SCRAPER's OPTIONS. A player who sets something and presses CLOSE loses it, while BACK keeps it (G02). The fix sits in this slice's base. Its effect reaches every page, and some `onFinalize` functions push a GUI (a reboot toast), so closing every page in turn needs reading before a stream is written (for the pause).

### Interaction: the LED page x `GuiSettings::save` x the quirks' `ledcontrol`

The page writes `system.cfg` through `SystemConf::set` (memory) and registers no save function, so `save()` returns early. The boot script reads the file. The colour survives a restart only if the player happens to close another settings page with something to save in the same session.

### Interaction: the relay option x `delete this` x `save()`

The option list's callback replaces its own page without `save()`. Every row on the page that applies on close is undone by a relay change, and ENABLE NETPLAY is the one a player notices (G08).

### Interaction: COPY TO FREE SLOT x `copyToSlot` x `loadGrid`

At the pin `copyToSlot` reports success over an unchecked copy (SS-4); T2's fix makes it truthful. The page then refreshes and rebuilds on success, which moves the cursor to the first tile. With T2's fix the page says nothing on `false`. Both paths need the page's own sentence (PL-016, #782's message).

### Interaction: Moonlight's SERVER IP x `popen` x `ParseServerIp`

The IP goes through a shell unquoted. The pairing reader writes a host it parsed back to `moonlight.host`, and the next command quotes it no better.

## 3.6 Time to play

From `docs/vm-qa-log.md`: interface to a game's first frame was 0.6 to 1.11 s in September's rows. It reads `Not measured` on 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-16, and nothing here built an image. This slice's pages sit on the launch path at two points:
- **The manager's PLAY from a slot** (`GuiSaveState::input`, `:418-428`) calls the run callback and deletes the page: no process, no file. The manager's opening (the constructor) builds the grid from the repository's list, which is the cost before the first frame of the page, not of the game. COPY's synchronous copy (L-13) is not on the launch path.
- **The game options menu** (X on a game) opens with five cached `exists` (`GuiGameOptions.cpp:41`, `:53-56`). On the guest it opened within the frame after the press (G06 g06-8); no figure was taken.

Neither number is measured for this slice; the integration image's `time-to-play` run is owed with the window of the fixes.



## The first reader's 04-analysis.md (whole)

# Analysis — M6.P2 T31, EmulationStation's other pages (#678)

The first reader's analysis (Opus 5.5), written after stages A and B, the guest window and the stage-C proofs, before the seat saw the packet. § Second opinion follows the seat. The leads are `02`'s (L-01 to L-27); every line is the pin's (`16dbac5211`).

## Executive summary

The fork's three pages are built to the house rules. The scrape page is a fourth-tier page with CANCEL and its own help bar. The save state manager draws its bar at 640x480, deletes on a worker, and refuses a write while the saves tree is busy. The Wi-Fi scan's executor is the argv model #1192 names. Every YES / NO box lands on NO. The French is complete.

What does not hold:
- **The base's second exit.** START (CLOSE on every settings page's bar) deletes the pages without `close()`, so no save function runs. A choice B keeps, START drops (L-01, guest and fixture). This is upstream's, in batocera's and ROCKNIX's masters alike, and it reaches the fork's cloud pages, which are all `GuiSettings`.
- **Pages that set without saving.** The LED colour never reaches `system.cfg` (L-02, fixture). A relay-server change replaces NETPLAY SETTINGS without `save()` and drops ENABLE NETPLAY (L-03, guest).
- **Moonlight**, ROCKNIX's own page:
  - the player's SERVER IP is run by the shell (L-05, fixture);
  - UNPAIR says the settings were cleared while removing nothing (L-06, guest and fixture);
  - its title is English in every language, and no map entry or check sees it (L-07, guest).
- **Silence where the fork works.** COPY TO FREE SLOT says nothing when it fails. A copy that worked leaves the same frame, the cursor back on START NEW GAME and the new tile off-screen (L-13, guest). A failing BIOS script reads as an empty check (L-12, guest). An all-errored scrape gives no why and no next step (L-09, fixture).
- **The BIOS page's bar.** BIOS CHECK draws no help bar under full-screen menus, so DETAILS and REFRESH are never offered (L-26, guest). Its French summary is English word order (L-11, guest).
- **The map.** BIOS CHECK and SCRAPING GAMES, the fork's pages, are in neither the map nor `## Not mapped` (L-24).
- **`GuiLoading`'s done flag** races its worker (L-22, ThreadSanitizer).

One brief lead is closed without a change: the LED parse is guarded by the token count. One is narrowed: `GuiKeyboardLayout.cpp:164` writes a scratch render under `/tmp`.

## Risk assessment

| Risk | Leads | Likelihood | Impact | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A choice made on any settings page lost on CLOSE | L-01 | certain on START (guest) | settings the player set are silently gone, the fork's cloud pages included | Medium |
| The LED colour reset at every restart | L-02 | certain on devices with the row (fixture) | a setting that never sticks | Medium |
| ENABLE NETPLAY undone by a relay change | L-03 | certain on that path (guest) | netplay stays off after the player turned it on | Low |
| A typed SERVER IP run by the shell | L-05 | needs a crafted string; the player's own | a command run as root from a settings row | Medium |
| UNPAIR says it cleared what it did not | L-06 | certain (guest) | a pairing the player believes gone is still there | Medium |
| COPY TO FREE SLOT silent both ways | L-13 | certain (guest) | a player cannot tell whether the copy happened; a failed one is invisible | Medium |
| A failed BIOS check reads as an empty one | L-12 | when `rocknix-systems` fails (guest) | no recovery offered for a broken check | Low |
| BIOS CHECK's keys never shown | L-26 | certain at 640x480 (guest) | DETAILS and REFRESH undiscoverable | Low |
| `GuiLoading`'s race | L-22 | every use; benign so far | undefined behaviour in the async primitive | Low |
| Moonlight's English title; the BIOS French order | L-07, L-11 | certain (guest) | a French player reads English or broken French | Low |

| Three back landings that act | L-19, L-20, L-21 | certain on those dialogs | B saves, discards or starts a flow | Low |

## Coverage boundary

- **Read whole:** the three fork-own files, `GuiSaveState.cpp`, `GuiScraperStart.cpp`, `GuiBios.cpp`, `GuiSettings.cpp`/`.h` and `GuiLoading.h`. **The fork's diff whole:** the other four fork-modified files. **At the leads' lines:** the upstream pages (§ Coverage names which).
- **Every site of every census:** the six generators read all 76 paths.
- **On the guest:** the image is M7's composition over the pin, so every guest verdict is the image's, with the steps in `02` § The guest cycle, at 640x480, in English and French. Not run: G10 (the viewer's timing), G11 (the stale read), the scrape page's finished frames (offline only), L-19's press.
- **Not this slice's:** `SaveState.cpp`'s `copyToSlot` (T2's), `AtomicFileUtil.cpp` (T21's), `ApiSystem.cpp`'s executors (T21's), `DisplayAspect.cpp` (T32-T37's), `OptionListComponent.h` and `GuiMsgBox` (es-core): read where a lead turns on them, not graded.
- **Not compiled here:** the host has no toolchain (`es-syntax-check` exit 2; `g++` stops at SDL). The five stage-C harnesses compile extracted functions and pass their controls.

## The questions

### Q1 The dialogs and where back lands

All 50 sites read whole (`02` § The dialogs). YES / NO lands on NO at 17 of 17, proved on the guest for DELETE GAME and the manager. Three landings act: CONTROLLER MAPPING's OK (L-19), the metadata editor's saving NO (L-20), and the two SAVE CHANGES? boxes' discarding NO (L-21). All are upstream's and recorded with #585 lines owed. A change of behaviour on an upstream surface outside the lanes is the maintainer's to open (D-UI-079), so they go to the pause. The NFC writer's WRITE / CANCEL lands on CANCEL. The manager's `busyNow` box, re-asked inside YES (`:457`), is the gate's second ask, correct. The scrape page's finished state is dismissed by any input (L-10).

### Q2 The rows, their stores, and the writes that can lose the record

- **The 23 switches** are ES-own keys in `es_settings.cfg`, the store their readers read.
- **The credential rows:**
  - the scraper's three secrets are masked;
  - PLAYER PASSWORD is not (L-04);
  - an untouched editor writes on OK (#1185, cited);
  - the mode is #1193's (cited).
- **Writes that lose the record:**
  - START drops every page's choices (L-01);
  - the LED page never saves (L-02);
  - a relay change drops ENABLE NETPLAY (L-03);
  - SCRAPER stores its default set on the first visit and cannot keep "none" (L-15);
  - a first visit rewrites `es_settings.cfg` twice with nothing chosen (L-16).
- **PL-031's nickname rows** read the builder's seed (`FileData.cpp:653-657`); the move is PL-031's.
- **No reboot flag** is set by any page.

### Q3 The interface thread

- **The process starts** are all on the interface thread with no bound:
  - Moonlight's 5 `executeScriptLegacy`, 3 `runSystemCommand` and its version `popen`;
  - the LED page's command at every slider step (L-02);
  - `GuiControllersSettings`' two.
- **COPY TO FREE SLOT** runs `copyToSlot` synchronously (L-13). On the guest's disk 64 MB took under a second; a card is not measured here.
- **The image viewer's first tile** walks the library (L-18).
- **The 18 `GuiLoading`** completions run on the interface thread over a raced flag (L-22), holding raw `this` (#1212's shape, T21's).
- **SCRAPER's `getSearches`** reads the page's components from the worker (L-17).
- **The hasher constructions** are #1170's and #301's (cited).
- **Time to play** (`03` § 3.6): the manager's PLAY and the options menu start nothing at the press.

### Q4 The reads, and the checks whose failure reads as success

- **The 12 cached `exists`:** their writers are the scraper, RetroArch and the player (`02` § The reads). The stale-read positive was not reached on the guest (G11 `CANNOT`).
- **Failures that read as something else:**
  - the BIOS page's empty vector (L-12, guest);
  - `isFlockHeld`'s non-ENOENT failure as a running sync (L-14, keep an eye: ES is root);
  - COPY's silence both ways (L-13, guest);
  - UNPAIR's false success (L-06);
  - the all-errored scrape (L-09);
  - the Bluetooth bulk delete's ignored result (L-33, cited).
- **The 9 `atoi`:** the LED page's seven are guarded; the key mapping editor's tab value and the update card's percent are benign.

### Q5 The words, the French and the help bars

- **The fourth SKIPPED** is for the pause (L-08): `SKIPPED - YOU CANCELLED IT` against D-UI-028's reserved reasons. The comment argues it, and no row exists.
- **The French:**
  - 706 of 706 strings have their French (`es-untranslated`);
  - the BIOS summary is English word order (L-11);
  - Moonlight's title is no msgid at all (L-07).
- **Upstream's words:** FAILED and HttpReq's text on the netplay page; Moonlight's sentence-case boxes.
- **The bar-less pages:** of the 8 `.cpp` files with no `getHelpPrompts`, all are `GuiSettings` pages or opened from one and inherit the menu's bar. The fork's BIOS CHECK defines `getHelpPrompts` and never draws it (L-26). The manager draws its own (#149), as does the scrape page.
- **Sizing:** no page here calls `getMenu().setSize(` (#540's shape absent).

### Q6 The commands and their readers

- **The SERVER IP** reaches `popen` unquoted, in three commands (L-05). The fix is `Utils::String::shellQuote`, T26's single quoting function, or the argv executor `WifiScanProcess.h` already models (#1192).
- **The LED values** are numbers formatted by `%u` (safe by shape). The Bluetooth and storage arguments are joined bare (T21's `:66`, safe by shape).
- **`GuiUpdate.cpp:213`'s percent reader** takes the text after `>>>`.
- **The spectator and relay session** are #776's.
- No `system(`.

### Q7 The map, the structure and the merge debt

- **The checker** sees 12 of 37 pages' titles. Twelve titles are in neither the map nor `## Not mapped`; dispositions are proposed in `02` § The titles, with BIOS CHECK and SCRAPING GAMES to be mapped. MOONLIGHT is invisible (L-07, L-24).
- **`GuiAutoScrape`** is not built, though the inventory lists it as shipped (L-23).
- **The four tab-strip pages** are recorded (#63).
- **The duplicate dialogs** are identical per family.
- **Merge debt:** 0 upstream changes to any slice file since `cada856d86`. The fork's 12 files are all one-file pages, so D-WORKFLOW-080's option (a) holds here.

## Renovation face

### Q1 Modernize

`GuiLoading`'s done flag wants `std::atomic<bool>` (L-22). The manager's COPY wants the worker DELETE already has (L-13), and the image viewer's index wants to be built off the interface thread (L-18).

### Q2 Standards

- **Confirmations:** three landings act (L-19 to L-21).
- **Help bars:** a full-screen page must draw its own bar (L-26).
- **French:** every `_()` string has its French, but the composition (L-11) and the raw title (L-07) break the standard.
- **Outcome words:** L-08 (the fourth SKIPPED) and L-09 (no why).

### Q3 Bugs

L-01, L-02, L-03, L-05, L-06, L-12, L-13 and L-26 are proved defects (guest or fixture). L-09, L-10, L-11 and L-22 are proved with lower stakes. L-14 is a keep-an-eye item.

### Q4 Package currency

The 64 upstream files are at `rocknix/master`'s bytes. Of the leads' upstream sides, batocera's master is the same at `GuiLoading.h` and the START handler (L-22, L-01); Moonlight's and the LED page are ROCKNIX's own. Nothing in the slice is behind its upstream.

### Q5 Efficiency

Moonlight's and the LED page's commands block the interface thread per press or per slider step. The image viewer's first open walks the library. COPY copies on the interface thread. None of these is on the launch path.

### Q6 Dead ends

- `GuiAutoScrape.*`: compiled by nothing, opened by nothing (L-23).
- The commented `addWithDescription` lines (`GuiFavoriteMusicSelector.cpp:45-46`) and the commented-out help prompts (`GuiKeyMappingEditor.cpp:476`) are dead text.

### Q7 Clarity and structure

`GuiSettings`' two exits should be one contract (L-01). BIOS CHECK and SCRAPING GAMES should be in the map (L-24). The pages' titles should be visible to the checker (#951/#965's evidence, `02` § The titles).

## Instruction File Recommendations

### Coverage gaps (would have prevented)

- **`es-native-ui.md` § Core patterns** ("Settings page ... `addSaveFunc` (apply-on-close)") does not say that START closes without applying. A line there would have kept L-01 from being a surprise.
- **`es-code-traps.md`, the window paragraph,** names the manager as the example of a page that draws its own bar. A checker of `renderHelpPromptsEarly` against full-screen `GuiComponent` pages would have caught L-26.

### Codification gaps (proposals for the coordinator)

- **A walk's key is sent once the screen is settled, and the focus is read in a frame before an action press.** Three passes of this window were void without it (the friction line).
- **The brief's runs use `grep -F` for literal C++ calls.** `popen (` and `_\("` both slipped past a basic-regex `grep`.

## Second opinion

Pending: the seat runs at stage C's end (GPT-6 Astra at `max`, one refutation call after its probe). This section records the plan, the call and the grading.

## Quality self-check

- Every figure in `02` is a tool's line; the composer asserts the counts, and every generator has a constructed negative.
- Every proved lead names its artifact: a guest step's file and frame, or a stage-C harness's FAIL-then-PASS pair.
- Void guest passes are recorded as void. No void pass is cited as evidence.
- Corrections to the brief and to this lane's own earlier statements are listed (`02` § Corrections; `00` § Window).
- Not done: G10, G11, the scrape page's finished frames on the guest.

## Context 2: the register rows

```text
| D-UI-021 | 2026-09-05 | **The tab strip is a focus stop: left/right switch tabs only while the highlight is on the strip; rows keep left/right and cycle in place.** Chosen over flattening the scraper page to one grouped list. `ComponentTab` already rendered a focused state that `MenuComponent` never let it reach — the strip sat in the grid as non-focusable and took every left/right on the page. Fixing the component means all five tab screens inherit it and the page keeps upstream's shape. Maintainer's call. | #65, #63 |

| D-CLOUD-053 | 2026-09-07 | **Every writer of the saves tree inside EmulationStation is gated by the transfer lock, and a deletion made from a menu is recorded.** Refines D-CLOUD-038 (the launch gate) and #22 R6. Maintainer: *"we might not want to let someone modify a game save or start the same game they're exiting, or any game for that matter, until cloud saves are complete. It could create a weird race condition if someone exits a game, it's backing up to the cloud, and then they try to delete a save or select a new one."* So: a game launch (shipped today for a sync ES started, `FileData::launchGame`; extended to every sync by D-CLOUD-038), the save-state manager's DELETE and the renumber it triggers (`GuiSaveState` → `SaveState::remove` → `SaveStateRepository::renumberSlots`, which knows nothing of the cloud today), and any later editor of saves check `L_T` first and refuse with the shipped running-sync message while a pass holds it — non-blocking, nobody waits (R6). And a deletion the player makes from the manager writes a `retired` entry (#21 R3), so the next pass propagates a decided deletion instead of asking an unexplained-absence question (D-CLOUD-037). The bytes already in flight were never at risk — capture seals an independent copy (D-CLOUD-034) — the race is over the tree the next pass reads. | #22 (R6), #21 (R3), #23; the maintainer's census session, 2026-09-07 |

| D-UI-028 | 2026-09-10 | **Four outcome words for every cloud surface, then a why, what is in place, and how to recover.** `COMPLETED`; `COMPLETED WITH GAPS - <what>` (some of the run succeeded and some did not -- never reported as FAILED and never as COMPLETED, D-CLOUD-077); `COULDN'T FINISH - <why>` (nothing succeeded and it is not a sentinel); `SKIPPED - <reason>` only for the two sentinels (`NO NETWORK CONNECTION`, `ANOTHER CLOUD SYNC IS RUNNING`) and the launch cancel (`A GAME WAS STARTED`). The why comes from a `>>> why <sentence>` protocol line the scripts print at the failure point (rclone's taxonomy stays in the log), else from a small rc table; the in-place clause is one per verb (`WHAT WAS SENT IS IN YOUR CLOUD. THE REST IS STILL ON THIS DEVICE.`, `NOTHING ARRIVED. THIS DEVICE IS AS IT WAS.`); the recovery clause names the surface that retries (`TRY AGAIN`, or the row). `COMPLETED SUCCESSFULLY`, `SUCCEEDED`, bare `FAILED`, `STOPPED`, every log path and exit code retire from the screen. The card uses its action line for in-place + recovery; the transfer page keeps its seven fitted lines (D-UI-024) and gains `A TRY AGAIN  B CLOSE` on line 7 when the run did not complete. The row line under a toggle reads the same words with commas. Stamps gain a third field, the why token, additively. Register-of-record: the vocabulary table in `es-native-ui.md`. Fork resolved from the EmulationStation audit's proposal (docs/audits/2026_09_10-graceful-degradation). | #105 |

| D-UI-030 | 2026-09-10 | **A cloud run passes or fails; `COMPLETED WITH GAPS` is removed from the vocabulary (reverses that part of D-UI-028).** Maintainer, on getting it from a manual sync: *"if you don't know what the gaps are, it's not very helpful for you and it just makes you more anxious and trust the system less because it's working kind of. You'd rather just know it couldn't connect or it could connect."* A run whose parts disagree reads `COULDN'T FINISH - <why>` with the failing part's why, on card, page and rows alike. What did move is still said truthfully -- the card's in-place clause, the page's line 4 -- so nobody is told nothing happened when something did. The stamps keep the `gaps` token so a log can still tell a partial run from a total one. ES `cdbe284a5`. | #111 |

| D-UI-039 | 2026-09-11 | **Settings do not stack in one window: a group that grows past a few rows nests behind a row of its own, and `docs/es-menu-map.md` is updated in the same change as every row added, moved or renamed.** Maintainer: "I don't want to start stacking too many settings in one window, so we may need to think about nesting some of what we're doing. This is one reason we should make sure we're updating our mapped mermaid or D2 file of everything we've done in EmulationStation." The map had last been touched 2026-09-09 and lagged five row changes; brought current the same day. | #134, #23 |

| D-UI-073 | 2026-09-16 | **A save state deletion in the manager is instant: the dialog closes and the tile vanishes at once; the retire, the unlink and the grid reload run in that order on one worker thread, one deletion at a time, and the manager reloads its grid when the worker is done.** Maintainer, on the reproduction in #205 (YES 0.59 s to the first changed frame on x86_64 against 0.35 s for a copy; a second on the RG SP): *"Let's go with option 1 with the mitigation steps you've outlined."* The rules the go covers: the worker holds paths and never a pointer to the page, which polls for completion in `update()` (the OAuth pages' use-after-free, 2026-09-05, is the failure this rules out); one queue, one job at a time, so two deletions never put two writers on the manifest (capture re-runs once when the manifest moves underneath it, a third overlap is discarded); the worker is joined at exit, so no joinable thread is destroyed. Rejected: a busy spinner (honest, still a second's wait); making the scripts cheaper (the interface still waits). Refines D-CLOUD-053, whose contract stands: the retire still precedes the unlink. Same crash window between the two as before. | #205; maintainer chat 2026-09-16 |

| D-UI-074 | 2026-09-17 | **When a worker job lands, the save state manager rebuilds its grid only if the disk disagrees with the page; a landed job that changed nothing on screen draws nothing.** Refines D-UI-073, whose "reloads its grid when the worker is done" rebuilt every tile a second after YES on the RG SP -- one frame of an empty sheet. Guest d, build 10, a frame every 83 ms: DELETE changes at 0.34 s (the tile going), 0.60 s (the sheet with no tiles) and 0.69 s (refilled); COPY TO FREE SLOT the same at 0.26 / 0.52 / 0.61 s -- the flash the maintainer saw (#207). The page records the files its tiles were built from and, when `completed()` moves, compares them with what the repository would show now (nothing whose file is gone, nothing still pending); the rebuild with the cursor kept is now the exception path and logs an INFO line, because the one case that reaches it is a deletion that did not take, and the tile coming back is the player's only sign. Rejected: dropping the poll (hides a failed unlink); refreshing the repository without rebuilding (the grid holds its pointers and refresh frees them). Fork resolved during #207's fix. | #207; ES `GuiSaveState::update` (fa4ada9d3) |

| D-UI-078 | 2026-09-21 | **Long work runs on a foreground page that owns the screen until it ends; the only way out while it runs is CANCEL, which says what cancelling means for that job (start over, or resume where the job supports it); backgrounding is for fast work only.** Reverses the fourth-tier shape of D-UI-060 and D-UI-070 (#186 PL-07, #187, rule commit `0462cf0141`) for the offline-achievements scan, the cloud transfer page and the scraper; the other background cards (index, content installer, OS update, Bluetooth) are classified per surface in #241. Maintainer, on the RG35XX SP at the scan page's PRESS B TO KEEP SCANNING IN THE BACKGROUND: *"we should only allow things to run in the background when they're fast. If it's something that's going to take longer time, like scraping or doing the RetroAchievements offline work, we should force that to be on a foreground page."* And on the way out: *"We could add a cancel button if we want to give them the option of backing out. That way, they would know explicitly that they would have to start over or resume if we can support that. Backgrounding is tricky because there are more edge cases we're introducing for what has to happen in order for them to then finish it, surfacing an alert when it finishes, etc."* The background shape had no end signal: a scan left with B reported only on the row that launched it, which the player had to go and find. | #241, #236, #187, D-UI-060, D-UI-070, D-UI-028, D-CLOUD-129 |

| D-UI-079 | 2026-09-22 | **D-UI-078's foreground-page rule applies to the lanes the fork works in -- cloud sync and cloud behaviours, game saves, Wi-Fi, the offline achievements work -- and not to upstream surfaces outside them: the game index (`ThreadedHasher`), the content installer, the OS update and the Bluetooth scan keep their background cards for now.** Maintainer, 2026-09-22, closing #241's last box: *"We don't need to alter behaviors outside of the core work we've been tackling when it comes to the game index content installer, OS update, and Bluetooth scan. Let's not alter that work quite yet. I'm more comfortable modifying work within the different lanes we've been working in around cloud sync, cloud behaviors, game saves, Wi-Fi, etc."* The scraper was named in D-UI-078 itself and is done. | #241, D-UI-078 |

| D-WORKFLOW-195 | 2026-10-24 | **An Extended seat whose answer the model's output ceiling cuts on the packet is run again on a trimmed packet for that seat -- the same model, effort and provider; the analyses and tables whole, the cited files as line-numbered excerpts with context, the ask bounded -- never a model or effort substitution and never the same packet again.** `04` § Second opinion says the seats saw different evidence; an item one seat saw only in excerpt carries `excerpt`, and a disagreement that could be the excerpt's doing is resolved from the artifact, not from either seat. A fork resolved during execution on T30 (#677): Argon at `high` spent about 57k of its 65.5k output tokens reasoning on a 438 KB packet three times (`finish=length`), where T3's 308 KB packet took 30k. The maintainer may reverse it by a new row. | #677, #798; `adversarial-council.md` § Council model floor; the T30 hold post of 10:04 UTC and the coordinator's answer the same hour |

| D-WORKFLOW-080 | **How does the interface work go upstream in pieces a person can review?** D-WORKFLOW-066 sent it as one PR because `GuiMenu.cpp` and `ApiSystem.cpp` carry every bucket's rows and a per-bucket split was called the artificial cut; a ROCKNIX developer closed that PR (200 files, 41,496 insertions: *"how do you expect us to be able to review this? please can you separate the changes and use less AI to submit PR's"*), which reverses the premise. Three ways, the maintainer's to choose: (a) move each bucket's code out of `GuiMenu.cpp` into files of its own first (a mechanical refactor that is itself one reviewable PR), then seven PRs of disjoint files by the review guide's buckets; (b) split the shared files by hunk, which `tools/pr-stack-check` cannot build by content and a rebase breaks; (c) fewer, still large PRs (mechanics first, then the cloud, then the rest), each carrying its share of the shared files whole. My recommendation is (a). | #333; raised 2026-09-29 |
```

## es-app/src/guis/GuiScraperRun.cpp @ 16dbac5211 (whole, 258 lines)

```text
    1  #include "guis/GuiScraperRun.h"
    2  
    3  #include "CloudText.h"
    4  #include "TextFit.h"
    5  #include "guis/GuiMsgBox.h"
    6  #include "Window.h"
    7  #include "ThemeData.h"
    8  #include "LocaleES.h"
    9  #include "Log.h"
   10  
   11  #include <cstdio>
   12  
   13  GuiScraperRun::GuiScraperRun(Window* window)
   14  	: GuiComponent(window), mBusyAnim(window, ""), mBackground(window, ":/frame.png"), mShownFinished(false)
   15  {
   16  	auto theme = ThemeData::getMenuTheme();
   17  	mBackground.setImagePath(theme->Background.path);
   18  	mBackground.setEdgeColor(theme->Background.color);
   19  	mBackground.setCenterColor(theme->Background.centerColor);
   20  	mBackground.setCornerSize(theme->Background.cornerSize);
   21  
   22  	const float SW = Renderer::getScreenWidth();
   23  	const float SH = Renderer::getScreenHeight();
   24  	setSize(SW, SH);
   25  
   26  	mTextFont  = theme->Text.font;
   27  	mSmallFont = theme->TextSmall.font;
   28  	mTitle    = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, _("SCRAPING GAMES"), theme->Title.font, theme->Title.color, ALIGN_CENTER);
   29  	mStatus   = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, _("PREPARING..."), mTextFont,  theme->Text.color,      ALIGN_CENTER);
   30  	mCounter  = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, "",                mSmallFont, theme->TextSmall.color, ALIGN_CENTER);
   31  	mActivity = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, "",                mTextFont,  theme->Text.color,      ALIGN_CENTER);
   32  	mDetail   = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, "",                mSmallFont, theme->TextSmall.color, ALIGN_CENTER);
   33  	mNote     = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, "",                mSmallFont, theme->TextSmall.color, ALIGN_CENTER);
   34  	mElapsed  = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, "",                mSmallFont, theme->TextSmall.color, ALIGN_CENTER);
   35  	mFooter   = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, "",                mSmallFont, theme->TextSmall.color, ALIGN_CENTER);
   36  
   37  	// One column, 0.78 of the width, every line fitted to it, and the rows
   38  	// stacked from the fonts' own heights -- GuiCloudTransfer's geometry,
   39  	// so the long-job pages of this app sit the same way on every panel
   40  	// (its constructor carries the derivation and the numbers per panel).
   41  	const float w  = SW * 0.78f;
   42  	const float cx = SW * 0.5f;
   43  	const float x  = cx - w / 2.0f;
   44  	mLineWidth = w;
   45  	for (auto& t : { mTitle, mStatus, mCounter, mActivity, mDetail, mNote, mElapsed, mFooter })
   46  		t->setSize(w, 0);
   47  
   48  	const float hT = theme->Title.font->getHeight();
   49  	const float hM = mTextFont->getHeight();
   50  	const float hS = mSmallFont->getHeight();
   51  	const float natural = 2 * SH * 0.05f + hT + 3 * hM + 4 * hS + 5.5f * SH * 0.02f;
   52  	const float fit = natural > SH * 0.9f ? (SH * 0.9f) / natural : 1.0f;
   53  	const float pad = SH * 0.05f * fit;
   54  	const float gap = SH * 0.02f * fit;
   55  	const float rT = hT * fit, rM = hM * fit, rS = hS * fit;
   56  	const float H = 2 * pad + rT + 3 * rM + 4 * rS + 5.5f * gap;
   57  	const float top = (SH - H) / 2.0f;
   58  
   59  	float y = top + pad;
   60  	mTitle   ->setPosition(x, y);
   61  	y += rT + 1.5f * gap;
   62  	mStatus  ->setPosition(x, y);
   63  	mCounter ->setPosition(x, y + rM);
   64  	y += rM + rS + gap;
   65  	mActivity->setPosition(x, y);
   66  	mDetail  ->setPosition(x, y + rM);
   67  	y += rM + rS + 0.5f * gap;
   68  	// 5. The spinner while it runs, the note once done, one row. No caption
   69  	// on the spinner: line 1 says what is happening.
   70  	mBusyAnim.setBackgroundVisible(false);
   71  	mBusyAnim.setSize(w, rM);
   72  	mBusyAnim.setPosition(x, y);
   73  	mNote    ->setPosition(x, y + (rM - hS) / 2.0f);
   74  	y += rM + 1.5f * gap;
   75  	mElapsed ->setPosition(x, y);
   76  	y += rS + gap;
   77  	mFooter  ->setPosition(x, y);
   78  
   79  	mPanelSize = Vector2f(w + SW * 0.06f, H);
   80  	mPanelPos  = Vector2f(cx - mPanelSize.x() / 2.0f, top);
   81  	mBackground.fitTo(mPanelSize, Vector3f(mPanelPos.x(), mPanelPos.y(), 0), Vector2f(-32, -32));
   82  }
   83  
   84  // While the scrape runs, back asks whether to cancel it (D-UI-078: the page is
   85  // sat in, and CANCEL is the one way out); every other press is refused,
   86  // since there is nothing to choose and a stray press should not dismiss a
   87  // page somebody is waiting on. Once it has finished, any button dismisses
   88  // it: the SCRAPER page it came from is right behind, and a new scrape
   89  // starts there.
   90  bool GuiScraperRun::input(InputConfig* config, Input input)
   91  {
   92  	if (!input.value)
   93  		return true;
   94  	const ThreadedScraper::Progress p = ThreadedScraper::progress();
   95  	if (!p.finished)
   96  	{
   97  		if (config->isMappedTo(BUTTON_BACK, input))
   98  			askCancel();
   99  		return true;
  100  	}
  101  	delete this;
  102  	return true;
  103  }
  104  
  105  // What cancelling means, read at the moment of deciding (D-UI-023): each
  106  // game's result is saved as it comes in, so what is scraped stays, and the
  107  // game lists show it once they are updated. YES first, NO last so B
  108  // answers NO. Nothing of the page is captured: the stop is a static call on
  109  // the run in flight, and the page shows the outcome as the run ends.
  110  void GuiScraperRun::askCancel()
  111  {
  112  	mWindow->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(mWindow,
  113  		_("CANCEL SCRAPING?") + std::string("\n\n")
  114  			+ _("WHAT'S SCRAPED SO FAR IS KEPT. UPDATE GAMELISTS TO APPLY IT."),
  115  		_("YES"), [] { ThreadedScraper::stop(); },
  116  		_("NO"), nullptr));
  117  }
  118  
  119  std::vector<HelpPrompt> GuiScraperRun::getHelpPrompts()
  120  {
  121  	std::vector<HelpPrompt> prompts;
  122  	const ThreadedScraper::Progress p = ThreadedScraper::progress();
  123  	if (!p.finished)
  124  	{
  125  		prompts.push_back(HelpPrompt(BUTTON_BACK, _("CANCEL")));
  126  		return prompts;
  127  	}
  128  	prompts.push_back(HelpPrompt(BUTTON_BACK, _("CLOSE")));
  129  	return prompts;
  130  }
  131  
  132  // The word for the run (D-UI-028): COMPLETED when every game was looked
  133  // at; SKIPPED for the player's own cancel (nothing went wrong, and the
  134  // games not reached are the next scrape's); COULDN'T FINISH when the
  135  // scraper refused the run -- the why on line 4 is its own words -- or when
  136  // games among many could not be scraped (a run whose parts disagree is
  137  // COULDN'T FINISH, D-UI-030).
  138  std::string GuiScraperRun::outcomeWord(const ThreadedScraper::Progress& p)
  139  {
  140  	if (p.cancelled)
  141  		return _("SKIPPED - YOU CANCELLED IT");
  142  	if (p.failed || p.errors > 0)
  143  		return _("COULDN'T FINISH");
  144  	return _("COMPLETED");
  145  }
  146  
  147  void GuiScraperRun::render(const Transform4x4f& parentTrans)
  148  {
  149  	Transform4x4f trans = parentTrans * getTransform();
  150  	Renderer::setMatrix(trans);
  151  	// Dim the whole screen: this is a takeover, and the carousel showing
  152  	// through would say the app is idle while a scrape is in flight.
  153  	Renderer::drawRect(0.f, 0.f, mSize.x(), mSize.y(), 0x000000D0);
  154  
  155  	mBackground.render(trans);
  156  
  157  	for (auto& t : { mTitle, mStatus, mCounter, mActivity, mDetail, mNote, mElapsed, mFooter })
  158  		t->render(trans);
  159  
  160  	// A spinner and never a bar: the scraper reports each game as it
  161  	// finishes it, and a game with a video to fetch takes as long as ten
  162  	// without, so a bar at i/n would be an invented position
  163  	// (es-native-ui.md: a bar only where a real percentage exists).
  164  	if (!mShownFinished)
  165  		mBusyAnim.render(trans);
  166  
  167  	// The keys are the help bar's, drawn here: with full-screen menus on
  168  	// (every handheld) Window draws no help while a second page is open, so
  169  	// the footer spelled the cancel out as PRESS B -- the wrong button on a
  170  	// swapped mapping (#308 8-es claude F-ES-05, 8a gpt F-ES-15;
  171  	// es-ui-style-guide.md, Interaction rules). As the transfer and scan
  172  	// pages do.
  173  	if (mWindow->peekGui() == this && Renderer::ScreenSettings::fullScreenMenus())
  174  		mWindow->renderHelpPromptsEarly(parentTrans);
  175  }
  176  
  177  // Clip to one line: a long game name gets an ellipsis, never a second line.
  178  // On characters, not bytes (TextFit; #308 8a gpt F-ES-13): a title ending in
  179  // an accented or Japanese character was measured, and could be shown, with
  180  // half a character on its end.
  181  std::string GuiScraperRun::fitOneLine(const std::shared_ptr<Font>& font, std::string text, float width)
  182  {
  183  	if (!font)
  184  		return text;
  185  	return TextFit::fitOneLine(text, width, [&font](const std::string& t) { return font->sizeText(t).x(); });
  186  }
  187  
  188  // "GAMES SCRAPED: 12  -  COULDN'T SCRAPE: 1" (the separator on screen is a
  189  // middle dot; ASCII here, es-code-traps.md) -- what this run did. A game the
  190  // scraper could not do (no match, a timeout) counts against the run and
  191  // is the next scrape's (D-UI-030).
  192  std::string GuiScraperRun::countsLine(const ThreadedScraper::Progress& p)
  193  {
  194  	const int scraped = p.done - p.errors > 0 ? p.done - p.errors : 0;
  195  	std::string line = std::string(_("GAMES SCRAPED:")) + " " + std::to_string(scraped);
  196  	if (p.errors > 0)
  197  		line += "  ·  " + std::string(_("COULDN'T SCRAPE:")) + " " + std::to_string(p.errors);
  198  	return line;
  199  }
  200  
  201  void GuiScraperRun::update(int deltaTime)
  202  {
  203  	GuiComponent::update(deltaTime);
  204  	mBusyAnim.update(deltaTime);
  205  	const ThreadedScraper::Progress p = ThreadedScraper::progress();
  206  	if (p.finished && !mShownFinished)
  207  	{
  208  		mShownFinished = true;
  209  		// The help bar changes with the page: CLOSE now.
  210  		updateHelpPrompts();
  211  	}
  212  	const int mins = p.elapsedMs / 60000;
  213  	const int secs = (p.elapsedMs / 1000) % 60;
  214  	char elapsed[32];
  215  	snprintf(elapsed, sizeof(elapsed), "%d:%02d", mins, secs);
  216  
  217  	if (p.finished)
  218  	{
  219  		// The same seven rows, now carrying the outcome (D-UI-028): 1 the
  220  		// word; 2 what this run did; 3 blank; 4 why it stopped, when the
  221  		// scraper said; 5 what to do next; 6 elapsed; 7 the buttons.
  222  		mStatus  ->setText(fitOneLine(mTextFont, outcomeWord(p), mLineWidth));
  223  		mCounter ->setText(fitOneLine(mSmallFont, countsLine(p), mLineWidth));
  224  		mActivity->setText("");
  225  		mDetail  ->setText(p.failed ? fitOneLine(mSmallFont, p.failure, mLineWidth) : "");
  226  		// Each result is saved as it comes in; the lists show it once they
  227  		// are rebuilt (the sentence upstream's toast ended on). The longest
  228  		// form that fits the line (D-UI-035): the French of the full
  229  		// sentence ran off a 640x480 panel and was cut mid-word.
  230  		std::shared_ptr<Font> small = mSmallFont;
  231  		mNote    ->setText(p.done - p.errors > 0 ? CloudText::chooseThatFits(
  232  			{ _("UPDATE GAMELISTS TO APPLY CHANGES."), _("UPDATE GAMELISTS TO APPLY IT.") },
  233  			mLineWidth, [small](const std::string& t) { return small ? small->sizeText(t).x() : 0.0f; }) : "");
  234  		mElapsed ->setText(std::string(_("ELAPSED")) + " " + elapsed);
  235  		mFooter  ->setText(_("PRESS ANY BUTTON TO CLOSE"));
  236  	}
  237  	else
  238  	{
  239  		// 1 what it is doing; 2 GAME i OF n; 3 the game it is on -- the line
  240  		// that says it is alive, because a video can take a while and a
  241  		// frozen count is indistinguishable from a hang; 4 the counts.
  242  		mStatus->setText(p.done > 0 || !p.game.empty() ? _("SCRAPING...") : _("PREPARING..."));
  243  		std::string counter;
  244  		if (p.total > 0 && (p.done > 0 || !p.game.empty()))
  245  		{
  246  			const int at = p.done + 1 <= p.total ? p.done + 1 : p.total;
  247  			counter = std::string(_("GAME")) + " " + std::to_string(at) + " " + std::string(_("OF")) + " " + std::to_string(p.total);
  248  		}
  249  		mCounter ->setText(counter);
  250  		mActivity->setText(fitOneLine(mTextFont, p.game, mLineWidth));
  251  		mDetail  ->setText(p.done > 0 ? fitOneLine(mSmallFont, countsLine(p), mLineWidth) : "");
  252  		mNote    ->setText("");
  253  		mElapsed ->setText(std::string(_("ELAPSED")) + " " + elapsed);
  254  		// 7. That it takes a while; how to cancel is the help bar's, on the
  255  		// player's own back button (render).
  256  		mFooter  ->setText(fitOneLine(mSmallFont, _("THIS CAN TAKE A WHILE."), mLineWidth));
  257  	}
  258  }
```

```text
    1  #pragma once

    3  #include "GuiComponent.h"

    9  #include <memory>
   10  #include <string>
   11  #include <vector>
   12  
   13  // The page a scrape runs on (fork #241, D-UI-078). A scrape asks the
   14  // scraper about every selected game and fetches its media -- minutes for a
   15  // library -- which is the fourth surface tier (es-native-ui.md): a page
   16  // that owns the screen, shows the live line and the elapsed time, stays
   17  // with the outcome until the player dismisses it, and whose one way out
   18  // while it runs is CANCEL. Upstream drew the run on a corner card and
   19  // ended it with a toast, both gone before anybody who walked away came
   20  // back. GuiOfflineScan's shape: the run is ThreadedScraper's, the page is
   21  // a view of it through ThreadedScraper::progress(), and the geometry is
   22  // the scan page's so the app's long-job pages sit the same way on every
   23  // panel.
   24  class GuiScraperRun : public GuiComponent
   25  {
   26  public:
   27  	GuiScraperRun(Window* window);
   28  
   29  	void render(const Transform4x4f& parentTrans) override;
   30  	bool input(InputConfig* config, Input input) override;
   31  	std::vector<HelpPrompt> getHelpPrompts() override;
   32  	void update(int deltaTime) override;
   33  
   34  private:
   35  	// The done page's word (D-UI-028), from how the run ended.
   36  	static std::string outcomeWord(const ThreadedScraper::Progress& p);
   37  	static std::string fitOneLine(const std::shared_ptr<Font>& font, std::string text, float width);
   38  	// "GAMES SCRAPED: n  -  COULDN'T SCRAPE: e" (a middle dot on screen; ASCII here)
   39  	static std::string countsLine(const ThreadedScraper::Progress& p);
   40  	// The CANCEL confirmation: what cancelling means, then
   41  	// ThreadedScraper::stop() on YES.
   42  	void askCancel();
   43  
   44  	BusyComponent mBusyAnim;
   45  	NinePatchComponent mBackground;
   46  
   47  	std::shared_ptr<TextComponent> mTitle;
   48  	// Seven lines under the title, the scan page's layout: the state, GAME
   49  	// i OF n, the game being looked up, the counts so far, the spinner's
   50  	// row, elapsed, the notice. Once the run is over the same rows carry
   51  	// the outcome, the counts, the why, what to do next.
   52  	std::shared_ptr<TextComponent> mStatus;    // 1. SCRAPING... / the outcome word
   53  	std::shared_ptr<TextComponent> mCounter;   // 2. GAME i OF n / the run's counts
   54  	std::shared_ptr<TextComponent> mActivity;  // 3. the game / blank once done
   55  	std::shared_ptr<TextComponent> mDetail;    // 4. the counts so far / the why
   56  	std::shared_ptr<TextComponent> mNote;      // 5. where the spinner was: what to do next, once done
   57  	std::shared_ptr<TextComponent> mElapsed;   // 6. elapsed
   58  	std::shared_ptr<TextComponent> mFooter;    // 7. the notice / the buttons
   59  	std::shared_ptr<Font> mTextFont;
   60  	std::shared_ptr<Font> mSmallFont;
   61  	float mLineWidth;
   62  
   63  	Vector2f mPanelPos;
   64  	Vector2f mPanelSize;
   65  
   66  	bool mShownFinished;
   67  };
```

```text
    1  #pragma once
    2  
    3  // A bounded, read-only subprocess for the Wi-Fi picker (#577). No shell,
    4  // GUI, singleton or callback is owned here. Kept separate so a disposable
    5  // process fixture can exercise the actual runner without linking ES.
    6  #include <atomic>
    7  #include <chrono>
    8  #include <string>
    9  #include <vector>
   10  
   11  #if !WIN32
   12  #include <cerrno>
   13  #include <csignal>
   14  #include <fcntl.h>
   15  #include <poll.h>
   16  #include <spawn.h>
   17  #include <sys/wait.h>
   18  #include <unistd.h>
   19  extern char** environ;
   20  #endif
   21  
   22  namespace WifiScanProcess
   23  {
   24  struct Answer
   25  {
   26  	int code = -1; // spawn/read/signal/timeout/cleanup failure is never empty success
   27  	std::vector<std::string> lines;
   28  };
   29  
   30  #if !WIN32
   31  class Child
   32  {
   33  public:
   34  	Child(pid_t child, int output) : pid(child), fd(output) {}
   35  	Child(const Child&) = delete;
   36  	Child& operator=(const Child&) = delete;
   37  	~Child() { finish(); }
   38  
   39  	bool finish()
   40  	{
   41  		if (finished)
   42  			return cleaned;
   43  		finished = true;
   44  		close(fd);
   45  		// Even a reaped leader may have descendants holding the pipe. The
   46  		// spawn creates this group; never signal ES's own process group.
   47  		cleaned = kill(-pid, SIGKILL) == 0 || errno == ESRCH;
   48  		if (!cleaned)
   49  			kill(pid, SIGKILL);
   50  		const auto deadline = std::chrono::steady_clock::now() + std::chrono::seconds(2);
   51  		while (!reaped)
   52  		{
   53  			const pid_t waited = waitpid(pid, &status, WNOHANG);
   54  			if (waited == pid)
   55  				reaped = true;
   56  			else if ((waited < 0 && errno != EINTR) || std::chrono::steady_clock::now() >= deadline)
   57  				return cleaned = false;
   58  			else
   59  				poll(nullptr, 0, 10);
   60  		}
   61  		return cleaned;
   62  	}
   63  
   64  	pid_t pid;
   65  	int fd;
   66  	int status = 0;
   67  	bool reaped = false;
   68  private:
   69  	bool finished = false;
   70  	bool cleaned = false;
   71  };
   72  #endif
   73  
   74  inline Answer read(const std::vector<std::string>& command, std::chrono::milliseconds timeout,
   75  	const std::atomic<bool>* cancelled = nullptr)
   76  {
   77  	Answer answer;
   78  #if !WIN32
   79  	const auto stopped = [cancelled]
   80  	{ return cancelled && cancelled->load(std::memory_order_acquire); };
   81  	if (command.empty() || timeout.count() <= 0 || stopped())
   82  		return answer;
   83  	std::vector<char*> argv;
   84  	for (const auto& part : command)
   85  		argv.push_back(const_cast<char*>(part.c_str()));
   86  	argv.push_back(nullptr);
   87  
   88  	int output[2];
   89  	if (pipe2(output, O_CLOEXEC) != 0)
   90  		return answer;
   91  	// File actions must not close stdout after dup2 when an inherited
   92  	// standard descriptor was absent and pipe2 reused its number.
   93  	for (int& descriptor : output)
   94  		if (descriptor <= STDERR_FILENO)
   95  		{
   96  			const int moved = fcntl(descriptor, F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC, STDERR_FILENO + 1);
   97  			if (moved == -1)
   98  			{
   99  				close(output[0]); close(output[1]);
  100  				return answer;
  101  			}
  102  			close(descriptor);
  103  			descriptor = moved;
  104  		}
  105  	posix_spawn_file_actions_t actions;
  106  	posix_spawnattr_t attributes;
  107  	if (posix_spawn_file_actions_init(&actions) != 0)
  108  	{
  109  		close(output[0]); close(output[1]);
  110  		return answer;
  111  	}
  112  	if (posix_spawnattr_init(&attributes) != 0)
  113  	{
  114  		posix_spawn_file_actions_destroy(&actions);
  115  		close(output[0]); close(output[1]);
  116  		return answer;
  117  	}
  118  	pid_t pid = -1;
  119  	const bool configured =
  120  		posix_spawn_file_actions_adddup2(&actions, output[1], STDOUT_FILENO) == 0 &&
  121  		posix_spawn_file_actions_addclose(&actions, output[0]) == 0 &&
  122  		posix_spawn_file_actions_addclose(&actions, output[1]) == 0 &&
  123  		posix_spawnattr_setflags(&attributes, POSIX_SPAWN_SETPGROUP) == 0 &&
  124  		posix_spawnattr_setpgroup(&attributes, 0) == 0;
  125  	const int spawned = configured && !stopped() ? posix_spawnp(&pid, argv[0], &actions, &attributes, argv.data(), environ) : -1;
  126  	posix_spawnattr_destroy(&attributes);
  127  	posix_spawn_file_actions_destroy(&actions);
  128  	close(output[1]);
  129  	if (spawned != 0)
  130  	{
  131  		close(output[0]);
  132  		return answer;
  133  	}
  134  
  135  	Child child(pid, output[0]);
  136  	const int flags = fcntl(child.fd, F_GETFL);
  137  	if (flags == -1 || fcntl(child.fd, F_SETFL, flags | O_NONBLOCK) == -1)
  138  		return answer;
  139  	const auto deadline = std::chrono::steady_clock::now() + timeout;
  140  	std::string outputText;
  141  	bool eof = false;
  142  	while (true)
  143  	{
  144  		if (stopped())
  145  			return answer;
  146  		char buffer[4096];
  147  		while (!eof)
  148  		{
  149  			if (stopped())
  150  				return answer;
  151  			const ssize_t count = ::read(child.fd, buffer, sizeof(buffer));
  152  			if (count > 0)
  153  			{
  154  				// A corrupt/unbounded command must not grow the UI's heap.
  155  				if (outputText.size() + count > 256 * 1024 || std::chrono::steady_clock::now() >= deadline)
  156  					return answer;
  157  				outputText.append(buffer, count);
  158  			}
  159  			else if (count == 0)
  160  				eof = true;
  161  			else if (errno == EAGAIN || errno == EWOULDBLOCK || errno == EINTR)
  162  				break;
  163  			else
  164  				return answer;
  165  		}
  166  		if (!child.reaped)
  167  		{
  168  			const pid_t waited = waitpid(child.pid, &child.status, WNOHANG);
  169  			if (waited == child.pid)
  170  				child.reaped = true;
  171  			else if (waited < 0 && errno != EINTR)
  172  				return answer;
  173  		}
  174  		if (eof && child.reaped)
  175  			break;
  176  		if (std::chrono::steady_clock::now() >= deadline)
  177  			return answer;
  178  		pollfd descriptor{eof ? -1 : child.fd, POLLIN, 0};
  179  		if (poll(&descriptor, 1, 25) < 0 && errno != EINTR)
  180  			return answer;
  181  	}
  182  	if (stopped() || !child.finish() || !WIFEXITED(child.status))
  183  		return answer;
  184  	answer.code = WEXITSTATUS(child.status);
  185  	if (answer.code == 0)
  186  	{
  187  		size_t start = 0;
  188  		while (start < outputText.size())
  189  		{
  190  			const size_t end = outputText.find('\n', start);
  191  			const std::string line = outputText.substr(start, end == std::string::npos ? end : end - start);
  192  			if (!line.empty())
  193  				answer.lines.push_back(line);
  194  			if (end == std::string::npos)
  195  				break;
  196  			start = end + 1;
  197  		}
  198  	}
  199  #endif
  200  	return answer;
  201  }
  202  }
```

```text
   10  #include "components/MultiLineMenuEntry.h"
```
